# Leads canvass, Nov. 5, 2026

The owner's question (Nov. 5, 2026): "Can we canvass other 'leads' to see if anything suggests we need to send a 'lead' back to a reader, because it seems like the entry could be written based on materials in-hand?" The rule, as restated that day (CLAUDE.md, "Leads"; `notes/briefs/reader.md` R18, R41, R44, R50): a lead is what cannot yet go into the calendar on a source in hand. An event a source in hand dates, however loosely, is an entry ("Check: the day" where the day is loose); a source in hand that disagrees with an entry is a variant or a "Check:"; an item passed over as minor is a row of Passed over with R18's ground.

Canvassed: every item of `notes/leads.md`; every "Leads" section of `notes/book-checks/*.md`; every Passed over row there whose ruling names a lead ("lead", "a lead", "to leads", "undated; lead" and the like). An item a book-check's Leads section shares with `notes/leads.md` (the merger's copy) is counted once, under `notes/leads.md`; a leads.md item that only restates its book-check's Passed over rows (a run of the rows' pages) is counted under the rows, and shown below marked so. Pointers ("the rows above marked 'a lead'"), headings and "(none this run)" lines are not counted.

The classes:

- **A**, a true lead: a source not in hand or refused, an event no source read dates, a figure no source read gives, a thing recalled. Left in place; not listed below.
- **B**, entry-writable now: a source the series has read (a book-check file exists for it, or the item cites it by page) or a record the series holds (FRUS, the Public Papers, the Record by its page tables, the Statutes, the Daily Digest, the *Bulletin*, the recordings' catalogues, the Sunday programs) dates the event, however loosely. Send back.
- **C**, a variant or Check: a source in hand disagrees with an existing entry, or with itself on an entry's point. Send back.
- **D**, minor (or out of the brief, or outside the span) by R18, but filed as a lead: to Passed over with R18's ground, for the owner to rule (R44).
- **E**, settled: the event is in the calendar now, or the item says so. Remove from the list.

How the sorting was done. The leads.md items and the book-checks' Leads bullets were read one by one and ruled by hand. The Passed over rows (3,974 ruled "lead" in one form or another) were sorted by rule, row by row: the ruling cell first (already in or entered: E; minor, private, a repetition, out of the brief, below the level, a passed-over paper: D; differs, a variant, not borne out: C), then the date column (a day, a month, a season, "early", "late", "after the convention": B; a year, a span of years, or none: A, unless the event cell itself carries a full date: then B). Every B and C row was then set against the calendar's tree (`lists/cal/`, 19,227 dated entries; the ledger empty today) on its day, a day either side, and its month: where an entry of those days shares three or more distinctive words with the row (names, titles, places), the row is ruled E, "probably already in", with the entry's id; where it shares two, the row stays B with "compare" and the id. Those two marks are a machine's and want a reader's eye; the rest of the B rows were not checked against the tree one by one, and some will turn out already in. Strictness: an item that names a read source with no page, or an event "to date from the sources his notes give," is A; "probably in X" is A.

**Totals (counted once):** A 1703, B 3370, C 51, D 111, E 182; 5417 items. Not counted: 178 (repeats, pointers, headings).

## D: minor, out of the brief or outside the span, filed as a lead (to Passed over with R18's ground)

R18 as it stands: "minor" is only a repetition with nothing new, private or social trivia, a unit's routine; another country's domestic politics is within the brief. Several of these rows were ruled on the older, wider sense ("out of the brief (German internal)", "a church body's resolution"), and by R18 now are entries: the owner rules. A row ruled both minor and a lead is split: the minor part to Passed over, the dated lead part as B.
